height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$3$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$8$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$8$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$6$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$6$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$9$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$8$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$8$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$9$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}=T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}2.

Theorem 5.4.3
.

The row-insertion of type
A\mathrm{II}
coincides with the Berele row-insertion
:

T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x=T\xleftarrow{\mathrm{B}}x\quad\text{ for all }(T,x)\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times[2n].

Proof.

Let
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
and
x\in[2n]
.
Set
T^{\prime}:=T\leftarrow x
,
S:=T\xleftarrow{\mathrm{B}}x
and
U:=T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x
.
Note that we have
U=P(T^{\prime})
by definition.
Let
(c_{1},\dots,c_{r})
and
(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})
denote the row-insertion route and the row-inserting letters for
(T,x)
, respectively.

First, suppose that
x_{i}\geq 2i-1
for all
i\in[r]
.
By Lemma
4.2.3
(
1
), we have

S=T^{\prime}.

This implies that
T^{\prime}
is symplectic.
Hence, Lemma
5.3.2
implies that

P(T^{\prime})=T^{\prime}.

Therefore, the assertion follows.

Next, suppose that
x_{i}<2i-1
for some
i\in[r]
.
Let
s
denote the minimum among those
i
.
Let
S^{\prime}
denote the punctured tableau of shape
(\nu,\{(s-1,1)\})
defined by

S^{\prime}(i,j):=\begin{cases}x_{i}&\text{ if }i\in[s-1]\text{ and }j=c_{i},\\
T(i,j)&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

for each
(i,j)\in D(\nu,\{(s-1,1)\})
.
By Lemma
4.2.3
(
2
), we have
S=\operatorname{Rect}(S^{\prime})
.
Then, Corollary
3.5.9
implies that

(3)

\displaystyle w_{\mathrm{row}}(S)\equiv w_{\mathrm{col}}(S^{\prime}).

Now, let us compute
U
.
Set
U^{\prime}:=\operatorname{suc}(T^{\prime})
.
Then,
w_{\mathrm{row}}(U^{\prime})\equiv\operatorname{red}(\mathbf{a})^{\mathrm{rev}}\cdot w_{\mathrm{col}}(T^{\prime\prime})
, where
\mathbf{a}
denotes the first column of
T^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime\prime}
the remaining part.
By Lemma
4.1.2
(
2
),we have

•

a_{s-1}=2s-3
,

•

a_{s}=2s-2
,

•

a_{i}\geq 2i-3
for all
i>s
.

Hence, we see by the definition that

\operatorname{rem}(\mathbf{a})=(a_{s-1},a_{s}).

Therefore,
\operatorname{red}(\mathbf{a})^{\mathrm{rev}}\cdot w_{\mathrm{col}}(T^{\prime\prime})=w_{\mathrm{col}}(S^{\prime})
.
By Proposition
3.2.4
and equation (
3
), we obtain

U^{\prime}=S.

This implies that
U^{\prime}
is symplectic, and hence,
U=U^{\prime}
by Lemma
5.3.2
.
Thus, we complete the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.4.4
.

Let
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
and
x\in[2n]
such that
T^{\prime}:=T\leftarrow x
is not symplectic.
Let
s
denote the minimum integer such that
T^{\prime}(s,1)<2s-1
, and
T^{\prime\prime}
the punctured tableau obtained from
T^{\prime}
by replacing the
(s-1,1)
- and
(s,1)
-entry with holes.
Then, we have

T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x=\operatorname{Rect}(T^{\prime\prime}).

Proof.

The assertion follows from the proof of Theorem
5.4.3
.
∎

6.
Applications

In this section, we use Theorem
5.4.3
to lift the Berele RS correspondence and Kobayashi–Matsumura RSK correspondence to isomorphisms of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules.
Also, we establish the dual RSK correspondence of type
A\mathrm{II}
.

In this section, we fix
\nu\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
.

6.1.
Berele Robinson–Schensted correspondence

Proposition 6.1.1
.

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

\mathrm{RI}^{A\mathrm{II}}:V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(1)\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\xi\subset\nu,\ |\nu/\xi|=1\end{subarray}}V^{\imath}(\xi)\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\xi\supset\nu,\ |\xi/\nu|=1\end{subarray}}V^{\imath}(\xi)

such that

\mathrm{RI}^{A\mathrm{II}}(b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{x})\equiv_{\infty}b^{\imath}_{T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x}\quad\text{ for all }(T,x)\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times[2n].

Proof.

By Proposition
5.2.2
, Theorem
5.1.1
(
1
), and Theorem
5.3.5
, we can consider the following composition of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphisms:

\displaystyle V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(1)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{For}\otimes 1}V(\nu)\otimes V(1)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{RI}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq 2n}\\
\mu\supset\nu,\ |\mu/\nu|=1\end{subarray}}V(\mu)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{LR}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq 2n}\\
\mu\supset\nu,\ |\mu/\nu|=1\end{subarray}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\xi\subseteq\mu\end{subarray}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\mathrm{Rec}_{2n}(\mu/\xi))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sum_{\xi}\pi_{\xi}}\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}V^{\imath}(\xi),

where the last homomorphism denotes the sum of projections
\pi_{\xi}
onto
V(\xi)
.
For each
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
and
x\in[2n]
, the element
b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{x}
is transformed by this homomorphism as follows modulo the equivalence relations
\equiv_{\infty}
at each step:

b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{x}\mapsto b_{T}\otimes b_{x}\mapsto b_{T\leftarrow x}\mapsto b^{\imath}_{P(T\leftarrow x)}\otimes Q(T\leftarrow x)\mapsto b^{\imath}_{P(T\leftarrow x)}=b^{\imath}_{T\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x}=b^{\imath}_{T\xleftarrow{\mathrm{B}}x};

the last equality follows from Theorem
5.4.3
.
This assignment gives rise to the bijection in Theorem
4.2.2
.
Hence, the assertion follows.
∎

Definition 6.1.2

(
[
Sun86
, Definition 8.1]
; see also
[
Ber86
, Lemma 1]
)
.

Let
\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
and
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
An
oscillating tableau
, of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and size
N
is a sequence

(\nu^{0},\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{N})

of partitions in
\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
satisfying the following:

(1)

\nu^{0}=\nu
,
\nu^{N}=\xi
,

(2)

either
\nu^{i-1}\subset\nu^{i}
or
\nu^{i-1}\supset\nu^{i}
for all
i\in[N]
.

(3)

|\nu^{i}|-|\nu^{i-1}|=\pm 1
for all
i\in[N]
,

The set of oscillating tableaux of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and size
N
is denoted by
\mathrm{OT}_{n,N}(\nu,\xi)
.

For each
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\in[2n]
, set

\displaystyle P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N}):=\begin{cases}T&\text{ if }N=0,\\
P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N-1})\xleftarrow{A\mathrm{II}}x_{N}&\text{ if }N>0,\end{cases}

and
Q(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N})
to be the sequence
(\nu^{0},\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{N})
of partitions defined by

\nu^{i}:=\operatorname{sh}(P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{i}))\quad\text{ for each }i\in[0,N].

Theorem 6.1.3
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

(1)

The assignment
(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N})\mapsto(P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N}),Q(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N}))
gives rise to a bijection

\mathrm{RS}^{\mathrm{B}}:Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times[2n]^{N}\to\bigsqcup_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}(Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)\times\mathrm{OT}_{n,N}(\nu,\xi)).

(2)

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

\mathrm{RS}^{A\mathrm{II}}:V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(1)^{\otimes N}\to\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\mathrm{OT}_{n,N}(\nu,\xi))

such that

\mathrm{RS}^{A\mathrm{II}}(b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w})\equiv_{\infty}b^{\imath}_{P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N})}\otimes Q(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{N})

for all
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\in[2n]
.

Proof.

The first assertion is essentially the Berele RS correspondence
[
Ber86
, Theorem 2]
.
The second can be proved by induction on
N
by using the first assertion and Proposition
6.1.1
.
∎

6.2.
Kobayashi–Matsumura Robinson–Schensted–Knuth correspondence

For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)
denote the set of weakly increasing words of length
l
with letters in
[2n]
.
We often identify
\mathrm{SST}_{2n}(l)
with
\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)
via row-reading:
T\mapsto w_{\mathrm{row}}(T)
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
, and
w=(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)
.
Set

P(T,w):=P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{l}).

For each
i\in[l]
, let
r_{i}\in\mathcal{R}
denote the terminal row of the Berele row-insertion for
(P(T,w[i-1]),x_{i})
.
By Lemma
4.2.7
(
1
), it is weakly decreasing.
Hence, if
j
denotes the number of barred letters in
(r_{1},\dots,r_{l})
and if we set
\nu^{\prime}:=\operatorname{sh}(P(T,w[j]))
and
\xi:=\operatorname{sh}(P(T,w))
, then we have

(4)

\displaystyle\nu\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\xi,\quad|\nu/\nu^{\prime}|=j,\quad|\xi/\nu^{\prime}|=l-j.

Set

Q(T,w):=(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi).

Conversely, given
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
satisfying the condition (
4
) and
S\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)
, one can find a unique pair
(T,w)\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)
such that

P(T,w)=S,\quad Q(T,w)=(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi).

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

(1)

The assignment
(T,w)\mapsto(P(T,w),Q(T,w))
gives rise to a bijection

Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)\to\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\nu\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\xi\\
|\nu/\nu^{\prime}|+|\xi/\nu^{\prime}|=l\end{subarray}}(Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)\times\{(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi)\}).

(2)

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(l)\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\nu\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\xi\\
|\nu/\nu^{\prime}|+|\xi/\nu^{\prime}|=l\end{subarray}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\{(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi)\})

that sends
b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w}
to
b^{\imath}_{P(T,w)}\otimes Q(T,w)
modulo
\equiv_{\infty}
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from the argument above.
In order to prove the second assertion, let us consider the following composition of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphisms in Theorems
5.1.1
(
2
) and
6.1.3
(
2
):

\displaystyle V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(l)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1\otimes\mathrm{RR}}V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(1)^{\otimes l}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{RS}^{A\mathrm{II}}}\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\mathrm{OT}_{n,l}(\nu,\xi)).

For each
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
and
w=(x_{1},\dots,x_{l})\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l)
, the element
b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w}
is transformed by this homomorphism as follows modulo the equivalence relations
\equiv_{\infty}
at each step:

b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w}\mapsto b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{x_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{x_{l}}\mapsto b^{\imath}_{P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{l})}\otimes Q(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{l}).

Note that
P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{l})=P(T,w)
and that
Q(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{l})
is of the form
(\nu^{0},\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{l})
such that

\nu^{0}\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{j}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\nu^{l}\quad\text{ for some }j\in[0,l].

The triple
(\nu,\nu^{j},\nu^{l})
is nothing but
Q(T,w)
.
Hence, we obtain a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism

V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(l)\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\nu\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\xi\\
|\nu/\nu^{\prime}|+|\xi/\nu^{\prime}|=l\end{subarray}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\{(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi)\})

which sends
b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w}
to
b^{\imath}_{P(T,w)}\otimes Q(T,w)
modulo
\equiv_{\infty}
.
The first assertion ensures that this map is an isomorphism.
Thus, we complete the proof.
∎

Definition 6.2.2

(
[
KoMa25
, Definition 4.7]
)
.

Let
\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
.
A
column-strict oscillating tableau
of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and depth
k
is a sequence

(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{k},\xi^{k})

of partitions in
\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
satisfying the following:

(1)

\xi^{0}=\nu
,
\xi^{k}=\xi
,

(2)

\xi^{i-1}\overset{\text{hor}}{\supseteq}\nu^{i}\overset{\text{hor}}{\subseteq}\xi^{i}
for all
i\in[k]
.

Let
\mathrm{CSOT}_{n,k}(\nu,\xi)
denote the set of column-strict oscillating tableaux of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and depth
k
.

Remark 6.2.3
.

The notion of column-strict oscillating tableaux was introduced in
[
KoMa25
]
under the name of
semistandard oscillating tableaux
.
As stated there, Lee
[
Lee25
]
had introduced the notion of semistandard oscillating tableaux in a different meaning.
Hence, we give them different names which clarify their different roles in the present paper.

Definition 6.2.4
.

The content of
U\in\mathrm{CSOT}_{n,k}(\nu,\xi)
is the sequence
c(U):=(l_{1},\dots,l_{k})
defined as follows:
if
U=(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{k},\xi^{k})
, then

l_{i}:=|\xi^{i-1}/\nu^{i}|+|\xi^{i}/\nu^{i}|\quad\text{ for each }i\in[k].

For each
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
l_{1},\dots,l_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
w_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l_{i})
with
i\in[k]
, set

P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k}):=\begin{cases}T&\text{ if }k=0,\\
P(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k-1}),w_{k})&\text{ if }k>0,\end{cases}

and
Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k})
to be the sequence
(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{k},\xi^{k})
of partitions defined as follows:

•

\xi^{0}:=\nu
,

•

(\xi^{i-1},\nu^{i},\xi^{i})=Q(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{i-1}),w_{i})
for each
i\in[k]
.

Theorem 6.2.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
l_{1},\dots,l_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

(1)

The assignment
(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k})\mapsto(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k}),Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k}))
gives rise to a bijection

\displaystyle\mathrm{RSK}^{\mathrm{KM}}:

\displaystyle Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l_{k})

\displaystyle\to\bigsqcup_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}(Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)\times\{U\in\mathrm{CSOT}_{n,k}(\nu,\xi)\mid c(U)=(l_{1},\dots,l_{k})\}).

(2)

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{RSK}^{A\mathrm{II}}:

\displaystyle V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(l_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes V(l_{k})

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\{U\in\mathrm{CSOT}_{n,k}(\nu,\xi)\mid c(U)=(l_{1},\dots,l_{k})\}

such that

\mathrm{RSK}^{A\mathrm{II}}(b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{w_{k}})\equiv_{\infty}b^{\imath}_{P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k})}\otimes Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{k})

for all
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
w_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{\leq}(l_{i})
.

Proof.

The first assertion is essentially the Kobayashi–Matsumura RSK-correspondence
[
KoMa25
, Theorem 4.17]
.
The second can be proved by induction on
k
and by using the first assertion and Proposition
6.2.1
.
∎

6.3.
Dual Robinson–Schensted–Knuth correspondence of type
A\mathrm{II}

For each
k\in[2n]
, let
(1^{k})
denote the partition consisting of
k

1
’s:

(1^{k}):=(\underbrace{1,\dots,1}_{k}).

Also, let
\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k)
denote the set of strictly decreasing words of length
k
with letters in
[2n]
.
We often identify
\mathrm{SST}_{2n}(1^{k})
with
\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k)
via row-reading:
T\mapsto w_{\mathrm{row}}(T)
.

Let
k\in[0,2n]
,
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
, and
w=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k)
.
Set

P(T,w):=P(T,x_{1},\dots,x_{k}).

For each
i\in[k]
, let
r_{i}\in\mathcal{R}
denote the terminal row of the Berele row-insertion for
(P(T,w[i-1]),x_{i})
.
By Lemma
4.2.7
(
2
), it is strictly increasing.
Hence, if
j
denotes the number of unbarred letters in
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
and if we set
\nu^{\prime}:=\operatorname{sh}(P(T,w[j]))
and
\xi:=\operatorname{sh}(P(T,w))
, then we have

(5)

\displaystyle\nu\overset{\text{ver}}{\subseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{ver}}{\supseteq}\xi,\quad|\nu^{\prime}/\nu|=j,\quad|\nu^{\prime}/\xi|=k-j.

Set

Q(T,w):=(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi).

Conversely, given partitions
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
satisfying the condition (
5
) and
S\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)
, one can find a unique pair
(T,w)\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k)
such that

P(T,w)=S,\quad Q(T,w)=(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi).

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Let
k\in[0,2n]
.

(1)

The assignment
(T,w)\mapsto(P(T,w),Q(T,w))
gives rise to a bijection

Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k)\to\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\nu\overset{\text{ver}}{\subseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{ver}}{\supseteq}\xi\\
|\nu^{\prime}/\nu|+|\nu^{\prime}/\xi|=k\end{subarray}}(Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)\times\{(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi)\}).

(2)

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(k)\to\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu^{\prime},\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}\\
\nu\overset{\text{ver}}{\subseteq}\nu^{\prime}\overset{\text{ver}}{\supseteq}\xi\\
|\nu^{\prime}/\nu|+|\nu^{\prime}/\xi|=k\end{subarray}}(V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\{(\nu,\nu^{\prime},\xi)\})

that sends
b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w}
to
b^{\imath}_{P(T,w)}\otimes Q(T,w)
modulo
\equiv_{\infty}
.

Proof.

The assertion can be proved in a similar way to Proposition
6.2.1
.
∎

Definition 6.3.2

(
[
Lee25
, Definition 2.4]
)
.

Let
\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
.
A
row-strict oscillating tableau
of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and width
l
is a sequence

(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{l},\xi^{l})

of partitions in
\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}
satisfying the following:

(1)

\xi^{0}=\nu
,
\xi^{l}=\xi
,

(2)

\xi^{i-1}\overset{\text{ver}}{\subseteq}\nu^{i}\overset{\text{ver}}{\supseteq}\xi^{i}
for all
i\in[l]
.

Let
\mathrm{RSOT}_{n,l}(\nu,\xi)
denote the set of row-strict oscillating tableaux of shape
(\nu,\xi)
, rank
n
, and width
l
.

Remark 6.3.3
.

The notion of row-strict oscillating tableaux was introduced in
[
Lee25
]
under the name of
semistandard oscillating tableaux
; see also Remark
6.2.3
.

Definition 6.3.4
.

The content of
U\in\mathrm{RSOT}_{n,l}(\nu,\xi)
is the sequence
c(U):=(k_{1},\dots,k_{l})
defined as follows:
if
U=(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{l},\xi^{l})
, then

k_{i}:=|\nu^{i}/\xi^{i-1}|+|\nu^{i}/\xi^{i}|\quad\text{ for each }i\in[l].

For each
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
k_{1},\dots,k_{l}\in[0,2n]
, and
w_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k_{i})
with
i\in[l]
, set

P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l}):=\begin{cases}T&\text{ if }l=0,\\
P(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l-1}),w_{l})&\text{ if }l>0,\end{cases}

and
Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l})
to be the sequence
(\xi^{0},\nu^{1},\xi^{1},\dots,\nu^{l},\xi^{l})
of partitions defined as follows:

•

\xi^{0}:=\nu
,

•

(\xi^{i-1},\nu^{i},\xi^{i})=Q(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{i-1}),w_{i})
for each
i\in[l]
.

Theorem 6.3.5
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
k_{1},\dots,k_{l}\in[0,2n]
.

(1)

The assignment
(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l})\mapsto(P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l}),Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l}))
gives rise to a bijection

\displaystyle\mathrm{dRSK}^{A\mathrm{II}}:

\displaystyle Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k_{l})

\displaystyle\to\bigsqcup_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}(Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\xi)\times\{U\in\mathrm{RSOT}_{n,l}(\nu,\xi)\mid c(U)=(k_{1},\dots,k_{l})\}).

(2)

There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{dRSK}^{A\mathrm{II}}:

\displaystyle V^{\imath}(\nu)\otimes V(1^{k_{1}})\otimes\cdots\otimes V(1^{k_{l}})

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{\xi\in\mathrm{Par}_{\leq n}}V^{\imath}(\xi)\otimes\mathbb{Q}(q)\{U\in\mathrm{RSOT}_{n,l}(\nu,\xi)\mid c(U)=(k_{1},\dots,k_{l})\}

such that

\mathrm{dRSK}^{A\mathrm{II}}(b^{\imath}_{T}\otimes b_{w_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{w_{l}})\equiv_{\infty}b^{\imath}_{P(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l})}\otimes Q(T,w_{1},\dots,w_{l})

for all
T\in Sp\mathrm{T}_{2n}(\nu)
,
w_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{2n}^{>}(k_{i})
with
i\in[l]
.

Proof.

The assertion can be proved by induction on
k
and by means of Proposition
6.3.1
.
∎
